# Clue eval: suggested rewrites

Generated by `pnpm eval-clues` on 2026-10-10. 415 questions have at least one suggestion.

These are the judge's suggestions, not checked facts. Check every claim against a source before a rewrite goes into the bank.

## The Sopranos (`the-sopranos`): bad

Clues 2 and 3 are plot summary and character names, and clue 1 opens with a bare 'It' and reads awkwardly.

**Clue 1.** Bare 'It' opening, and 'driving home through the Turnpike' reads awkwardly.

- Now: It opens each episode with its lead driving home through the New Jersey Turnpike.
- Suggested: This crime drama's creator, David Chase, modeled its lead's cold, scheming mother on his own.

**Clue 2.** A plot summary of the show's best-known premise.

- Now: In it, a mob boss juggles running his crime family with panic attacks and visits to a therapist.
- Suggested: This crime drama's opening credits follow its lead out of the Lincoln Tunnel and up the New Jersey Turnpike.

**Clue 3.** Naming Tony and Carmela is instant, and 'a gang of gangsters' is clumsy.

- Now: In it, Tony leads a gang of gangsters while worrying about his wife Carmela and his teenage kids.
- Suggested: This crime drama's mob boss secretly sees a psychiatrist after he starts having panic attacks.

**Clue 1.** An egg-laying mammal with a venom spur is its best-known fact, so clue 1 is instant; it also opens with a bare 'It'.

- Now: It is one of the only mammals that lays eggs, and the males carry venom in a spur on each hind leg.
- Suggested: This animal glows blue-green under ultraviolet light, which scientists only reported in 2020.

## Sloth (`sloth`): needs_work

Good facts, but clue 1's 'moves so little' all but names the answer and it needs 'this mammal'.

**Clue 1.** Opens with a bare 'It', and 'moves so little' all but names the answer.

- Now: It moves so little that green algae grows in its fur, tinting it mossy — handy camouflage in the canopy.
- Suggested: Green algae grows in this mammal's fur, tinting it a mossy color that helps it hide in the treetops.

## Tennis (`tennis`): needs_work

Great opening clues, but the optic-yellow ball clue is an instant giveaway at clue 3.

**Clue 3.** Optic-yellow balls name the sport at once.

- Now: This sport's balls switched from white to 'optic yellow' in 1972, after research showed they were easier to see on television.
- Suggested: This sport's oldest major tournament began in 1877, when 22 men entered and about 200 people watched the final.

## The Scream (`the-scream`): needs_work

Strong facts throughout, but clue 1 is too direct for the opening clue and clue 4 is a fragment.

**Clue 1.** The word 'shriek' and the covered ears make it more direct than clue 2, and the diary does not actually describe the figure's hands.

- Now: The figure is not the one making the noise — the artist's diary has it clapping its hands over its ears against a shriek passing through nature.
- Suggested: A tiny note scrawled in the sky of this painting's 1893 version, saying only a madman could have painted it, was confirmed in 2021 to be in the artist's own hand.

**Clue 4.** Written as a fragment rather than a sentence about the answer.

- Now: Edvard Munch's 1893 picture of a wavy skull-faced figure on a bridge, hands clamped to its head.
- Suggested: This 1893 painting by Edvard Munch shows a skull-faced figure on a bridge, hands pressed to the sides of its head.

## Wi-Fi (`wi-fi`): needs_work

Four strong, distinct facts; only clue 1's bare 'Its' and clue 4's fragment need fixing.

**Clue 1.** Opens with a bare 'Its' instead of saying what kind of thing this is.

- Now: Its name was dreamed up by a branding agency and does not stand for anything.
- Suggested: This technology's name was dreamed up by a branding agency and, despite a popular belief, does not stand for anything.

**Clue 4.** Written as a fragment rather than a sentence.

- Now: The wireless networking standard that links laptops and phones to your router.
- Suggested: This wireless networking standard links laptops and phones to your home router.

## Jurassic Park (`jurassic-park`): good

Sound effects, rights deal, box-office record and plot make four clear angles in a good order; only a small wording fix in clue 4.

**Clue 4.** Saying 'island of Isla' repeats itself, since isla means island.

- Now: In this film, dinosaurs brought back by industrialist John Hammond's scientists break loose on the fictional island of Isla Nublar.
- Suggested: In this film, dinosaurs brought back by industrialist John Hammond's scientists break loose on Isla Nublar, a fictional island off Costa Rica.

## Blood (`blood`): good

Varied, specific facts in a good order; clue 4's wording could be smoother.

**Clue 4.** 'Makes this red fluid red' reads awkwardly.

- Now: Iron makes this red fluid red, and it courses through the arteries and veins.
- Suggested: Iron in its hemoglobin gives this fluid its red color as it courses through the arteries and veins.

## Frozen (`frozen`): good

Strong, specific behind-the-scenes facts; only clue 4's wording could be tightened.

**Clue 4.** The film does not turn things to ice; Elsa does, so the sentence is slightly loose.

- Now: Loosely based on 'The Snow Queen,' this animated blockbuster turns everything Elsa touches to ice.
- Suggested: Loosely based on 'The Snow Queen,' this animated blockbuster follows Elsa, a queen whose touch turns things to ice.

## Superman (`superman`): good

Three surprising, specific facts in a sensible order; the only polish is to drop the leaping echo from clue 4.

**Clue 4.** Its leaping detail echoes clue 3; another detail would keep the clues fully separate.

- Now: Faster than a speeding bullet, this caped hero wears a red 'S' and can leap tall buildings.
- Suggested: This caped hero, secretly mild-mannered reporter Clark Kent, wears a red 'S' on his chest.
